# Master-only ledger

Every commit `master` has that `callgrind-20g` does not, oldest first: `git log --reverse callgrind-20g..master`. 1134 commits from merge base `d520c9fd`. `master` is at `975b3f05` and `origin/master` at `e32fd95d`. Dates are author dates. Updated 2026-10-18.

Pinned review base: `callgrind-20g` at `1146b4f6`. The replay and the overlap are from that commit. The WORTH rows, the ports and the premise checks were run at `165fb8b1`, six commits behind it. Reading the hunks since then: the code those checks and the HAVE claims for 89, 97 and 115 rest on is unchanged (`project.rs` gained one line), but the analysis path the 143 tests run through changed, so that premise is unconfirmed at the pinned base. None of the eight WORTH commits touches a file those six commits changed, and nothing under `syntax`, `types` or `phpdoc` changed, so the code the WORTH rows rest on is the same at the pinned base. The fixture passes for 29 and 981 run through `project/session.rs`, which those commits changed, so they are unconfirmed there; row 108's oracle did not run at all. A reason that names `cbd9af81` says where its probe ran, an earlier commit of this branch than `165fb8b1`.

Progress: 1134 of 1134 judged (WORTH 8, ADAPT 264, SKIP 838, HAVE 24); 0 OPEN. Replay: 88 apply as text, 1046 do not, 0 are empty. 615 touch files `callgrind-20g` also changed.

- **applies**: whether the commit's patch applies as text to `callgrind-20g`, after every earlier commit that applied. `yes`, `no` (the cause is in the reason) or `empty`. Applying says nothing about compiling or passing tests.
- **overlap**: how many of the commit's files `callgrind-20g` changed since the merge base.
- **status**: `WORTH` applies as text (`git apply`, not a three-way merge) to the pinned base, compiles with no warnings, and the tests that exercise the change pass there. A row whose tests cannot run on this machine is WORTH only with the gap named in its reason. `ADAPT` is worth having but does not apply as text (it needs a three-way merge, a port or a rewrite), so it is rewritten here; the reason says what the port changes. `SKIP` is not worth having here, or is superseded, or its premise is absent; the reason says which. `HAVE` means this branch already has the change. `OPEN` is not judged yet; a reopened row keeps its earlier verdict in the why column as `reopened from SKIP: ...`.

## Decisions

- **Effects.** Each effect-soundness fix is ported into the demand-driven model (`registry/demand.rs`), starting with a fixture. Master's effects engine is not migrated.
- **Arena and typed ids.** Not migrated. A fix from these rows is ported only where its bug reproduces on this branch.
- **Validation and dump tooling.** SKIP: the branch keeps its own gates (`xtask`) and fixtures. Rows that are fixes rather than tooling keep the verdict their reason gives.
- **php-semantics track.** Master deferred the php-semantics research track and the PHP catalog at row 176 (`f8f0031d`). The 212 SKIPs that rested on that deferral were re-judged one by one: each is SKIP because the branch has no consumer for the code, data or document it adds, and each reason names what would bring it back. Whether the track is taken up at all is a decision for its owner, not a row verdict.
- **PHP-version syntax.** Rows 105, 106 and 107 are ported together and ship opt-in, not on by default.
- **Gate store.** Row 119 is split, not migrated as is: snapshots stay in the shared store, and the scratch paths move to each checkout's `.corpus-runs/scratch`, which `.gitignore` must ignore.

## Still open

- The per-row ports: effects soundness, the arena reproduction checks, the PHP-version rows (105, 106, 107), and the gate store's split (119) in the main checkout.
- The php-semantics track decision: if the catalog is taken up, its rows are re-judged as ADAPT for porting.
- Re-read in this pass, from each commit's diff and the branch's code (no compile): the 511 rows of the perf (128), fix (102), feat (47), refactor (22), test (16), misc (29) and no-prefix (167) groups, and the 411 ungrouped rows. Of the ungrouped rows, 302 touch only markdown: master's plan, ledger and work-stack notes (SKIP), the recorder plan's notes (rows 788 and 791, ADAPT with row 32), the corpus README (row 25, ADAPT with row 119) and a deletion the branch already has (row 992, HAVE). The other 109 touch code, data or fixtures, and each was checked against the branch's version of the file it changes, or against its absence. The recorder family (tools/builtin-runs, its tables and its scan files) is judged as one under row 32. The effects family and the arena and id routes are judged by the Effects and Arena decisions and a premise check (the branch lacks the file or mechanism, or still has the pre-fix code): their bugs are not reproduced here, and each port starts with its fixture. The other 212 rows keep the verdicts of the php-semantics re-judgment (e4a565e6). An ADAPT row whose replay says it applies as text gives a reason it is not taken as it is: a dependency absent here, a fixture for work it does not carry, a decision left to its owner, or a step not run here (a build or a round-trip check, which WORTH needs).
- Row 160 changes the level model (each finding carries its found-from levels; acceptance answers the lowest level that rejects), not performance. Rows 731 and 736 follow it. Whether the configured-level dependence the branch has (`RuleLevelHelper`) is wanted is the owner's call before any of the three is ported.
- The Effects decision names `registry/demand.rs` as the port target, but demand.rs answers purity and property writes only. The storage-level fixes (by-reference captures, globals, destructors, superglobals, element facts) need a target in the checker's own effect handling, which the branch has not decided. Their rows are ADAPT on the decision, not on a checked port target; the owner picks the target before any of them is ported.
- The recorder family (tools/builtin-runs, row 32 and the rows after it) is one port: taking row 32 takes the recorder, and its table is regenerated rather than copied. Whether the branch takes the recorder at all is the owner's call; until it is, these rows are ADAPT.
- Row 985 (the erp-web gitlink): the owner decides whether the erp-web corpus round in ROADMAP.md stays. That decides whether the gitlink and its .gitmodules entry go.
- Row 937 (Linux CI and the release legs on ubuntu-26.04): moving the release legs moves the glibc the binaries need, which the commit's own note puts at 2.43 or newer. The owner decides that floor before the row is taken.
